# P5 - Event Capture Hook
Paste into a fresh Claude Code session started in the Jarvis vault: `Read and run 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P5 - Event Capture Hook.md. Follow Hard Rules and Report Back exactly.`
## Why This Exists
Layer L0 of [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Loop Design|Conversation Loop Design]]: one deterministic, model-free script that writes a JSON line per prompt, tool call, tool failure, permission denial, subagent stop, compaction, and instruction load, so the weekly review reads facts and not scraped transcripts. This is build B3. Read the P3 postmortem first: if it changed the record shape, follow P3 over this note.
- **Verified in the hooks reference, re-read 2026-10-22:** 33 events. Common stdin fields: `session_id`, `prompt_id` (absent until the first user input), `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `permission_mode`, `effort`, `hook_event_name`. Event fields: `UserPromptSubmit` `prompt`; `PostToolUse` `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`, `tool_response`, `duration_ms`; `PostToolUseFailure` adds `error`, `is_interrupt`; `PermissionDenied` `reason`; `SubagentStop` `agent_id`, `agent_type`, `agent_transcript_path`, `last_assistant_message`; `PreCompact` and `PostCompact` `trigger`; `InstructionsLoaded` `file_path`, `memory_type`, `load_reason`; `Stop` `last_assistant_message`.
- **Verified in the same read:** matching hooks run in parallel; `PostToolUse` fires concurrently for parallel tool calls; async hooks cannot block or change Claude's behavior; the same handler defined in several settings files runs once; stdout of `UserPromptSubmit` hooks is added to context, so it must stay empty; `-p` runs kill async hooks at teardown.
- **Verified on the machine 2026-10-22:** Node v24.19.0 is installed. `.gitignore` and `.stignore` list specific log names only, so `events-*.jsonl` is not ignored yet and must be added.
- **Owner decisions 2026-10-22:** raw events stay per machine; scope is tracked projects only (the list confirmed in P1), which keeps The Plan's personal content out of Jarvis logs; no personal-life or pasted-document text is stored.
- **Not verified, step 1 does it:** the field names at runtime on Claude Code 2.1.309, and whether a slash command fires `UserPromptSubmit`.
## Hard Rules
- Scope is the Acer, Windows side, Claude Code only. Do not touch WSL, the Dell, Cowork, Cursor, or Codex.
- Do not edit `30_Order/Standards`, `Templates`, or `Workflows`. Build in `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/` (the script and a `lib/` folder) and its tests beside the existing `test-*.ps1` files.
- The script prints nothing to stdout, ever. It exits 0 on every input. It makes no model call and no network call.
- It never stores a `tool_response` body (byte length only), a full shell command (first token and ok state only), a pasted-looking prompt (over 2,000 characters or 40 lines: keep length, the first 200 characters, and `pasted: true`; these two limits are working rules, not from a source), or any text from a project outside the scope file.
- Redact stored text with the same eight rules as the exporter (`sk-ant-`, `sk-`, `ghp_`, `xox*-`, `AKIA`, Bearer tokens, `SetEnvironmentVariable` values, long mixed tokens). Write the rules once, in a Node module with fixture vectors in a test.
- One `fs.appendFileSync` of one line under 4 KB per event. `JARVIS_EVENTS_OFF=1` disables the script entirely.
- Never read or print `.credentials.json`, `.env*`, tokens, or `history.jsonl` text. Counting `history.jsonl` lines for a coverage test is allowed.
- Edit only the `hooks` block of `~/.claude/settings.json`, with targeted edits, after copying the file to the scratchpad. Do not touch the existing `Stop` and `SessionEnd` entries.
- Do not run `git commit` or `git push`. After any vault write, look for `.sync-conflict-*` copies.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, patch by heading, update `updated:`.
- Do not set the status of this prompt to `complete`.
## Implementation Order
1. **Key capture.** Register a temporary logger that records only field names and value types per event for about one hour of normal use. Compare with the documented fields above, answer the slash-command question, then remove the logger. Pin the observed field lists as fixtures in `test-events-schema.js` so a release that renames a field fails a test.
2. Write `hooks/jarvis-events.js` and `hooks/lib/`: parse stdin, drop out-of-scope projects (scope file `hooks/events-scope.json`, one cwd prefix per tracked project), classify, redact, and append. Common row: `{v, ts, machine, session_id, prompt_id, agent_id, event, cwd, project}`. Event fields follow [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Per-Prompt Record|Per-Prompt Record]]. On `Stop` also write a `turn_end` row with the first 300 redacted characters of `last_assistant_message`, `claims_done` (a regex list in a JSON config), and `verified_after_last_edit` (any Bash or PowerShell row with a command head in a test, lint, or build list, after the last Edit or Write row of this `prompt_id`; the list is a JSON config the owner can edit).
3. Failures: a parse error or unknown payload writes `{event: "failure", stage, message}` (message truncated) to the same file and a line to stderr. If the file cannot be written, append to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\claude-capture\events-fallback.log`. Silence is never an outcome.
4. Tests (`node --test`, fixtures are synthetic): each event type produces the expected row; stdout is empty for every event; garbage and empty stdin give a failure row and exit 0; an out-of-scope cwd writes nothing but a counter row; redaction vectors; a 50-process concurrent run leaves 50 intact lines.
5. Measure overhead: 100 sequential invocations, p50 and p95 wall time including Node startup. Report them. If p95 is over 150 ms, investigate before registering (a working ceiling, not a sourced one).
6. Register, after the tests pass: one handler, `node "<abs path>/hooks/jarvis-events.js"`, with `"async": true` and `"timeout": 10`, for `UserPromptSubmit`, `PostToolUse`, `PostToolUseFailure`, `PermissionDenied`, `SubagentStop`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `InstructionsLoaded`, `StopFailure`, and a second `Stop` entry. Leave every existing entry as it is.
7. Add ignore lines for `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/logs/events-*.jsonl` and the fallback to `.gitignore` and to `.stignore` (with the `(?d)` prefix, like the neighbors). Prove it with `git check-ignore -v`. Note that `.stignore` does not sync: the Dell needs the same line by hand in build B9.
8. Owner smoke test, written for them: open a fresh session in a tracked project, send one prompt that uses two tools, then run the counting one-liner you provide. It prints row counts per event for the session and the `history.jsonl` prompt count for that session id, never any text.
9. Patch [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]], [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Per-Prompt Record|Per-Prompt Record]] (the ignored-folder claim), and the hook table in `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/README.md`, each with a dated entry.
## Report Back
- The key-capture result: any documented field that was missing or renamed, and the slash-command answer.
- Test output, the overhead table, and the `git check-ignore` output.
- The `settings.json` hook-block diff and backup path.
- The smoke-test one-liner and, if the owner ran it, its counts.
- Anything you cut from the record and why.
## What Not To Do
- Do not print anything to stdout.
- Do not store prompt text from outside the scope file, or any `tool_response` content.
- Do not launch the hook through PowerShell. Node starts faster.
- Do not register for events not listed above.

# P6 - Weekly Digest
Paste into a fresh Claude Code session started in the Jarvis vault: `Read and run 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P6 - Weekly Digest.md. Follow Hard Rules and Report Back exactly.`
## Why This Exists
Layer L1 of [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Loop Design|Conversation Loop Design]]: a script that turns a week of event rows into the note the weekly review opens first, with zero tokens. Without it, steps 1, 2, and 6 of [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Weekly Review Protocol|Weekly Review Protocol]] stay manual. This is build B4. The throwaway counting scripts from the research were not kept, so everything here is rewritten with tests.
- **Verified 2026-10-22:** [[30_Order/Standards/Generated Output Standard|Generated Output Standard]] wants `type: generated`, `generator`, and `gen_kind` (`report` fits), no `status`, and `review_state` optional; the generated zone path is not decided and placement checks are waived until the registry sets one.
- **Verified in the hooks reference:** `transcript_path` may lag the live turn, so context sizes read from a transcript for a prompt that just ended can be one turn stale. The digest runs on whole days, so this matters only for the current day.
- **Depends on P5:** event rows exist for at least a few days. If P3 changed the record shape, follow P3.
- **Owner decisions 2026-10-22:** the human does the weekly blind pass; the digest syncs between laptops, so it carries ids and links and no prompt text. `agent_lines_kept_7d` is deferred unless P3 kept it.
- **Assumed, check:** an installed skill or subagent is a directory or file under `.claude/skills` and `.claude/agents`, so "available but never used" can be computed by listing them.
## Hard Rules
- Scope is the Acer, Windows side, Claude Code only, tracked projects only.
- Do not edit `30_Order/Standards`, `Templates`, or `Workflows`. Build in `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/` with tests beside it.
- Deterministic and model-free. The same events and the same seed give a byte-identical digest. No network.
- The digest contains prompt ids, session ids, counts, tool and file paths relative to the vault, and links to session notes. It contains no prompt text, no `last_assistant_message` text, and no pasted content.
- `history.jsonl` is read only to count prompts per session and day. Never copy a line of it.
- A missing field is tolerated and counted. Never crash on a version change; the digest lists the Claude Code versions seen.
- One writer per generated note: overwrite the week's digest in place, idempotently. Do not run `git commit`; look for `.sync-conflict-*` after writing.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, patch by heading, update `updated:` on notes you patch.
- Do not set the status of this prompt to `complete`.
## Implementation Order
1. Read [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Failure Taxonomy|Failure Taxonomy]] and [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Per-Prompt Record|Per-Prompt Record]]. Define each rule flag as code with a JSON config for thresholds: E1 retry loop (the same tool with a near-identical target three or more times in a row), E2 context bloat (the largest tool results and files read twice; context size per prompt from the transcript's last deduplicated `usage` before the prompt), E3 instruction not loaded (the `InstructionsLoaded` rows against a list of files expected for the project), E4 permission friction (denials), E5 capture gap (no event rows for a prompt that exists in `history.jsonl` counts), S6 execution error (a tool error with no change of approach), S7 false self-report (`claims_done` true and `verified_after_last_edit` false). Thresholds are working rules, not sourced; write them in the config with a comment saying so.
2. Write the digest script (`scripts/build-conversation-digest.js` or the language you choose, with a short reason). Input: an ISO week, the events file, the `history.jsonl` counts, and transcripts for context size. Output: `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Conversations/Digests/Digest <ISO week>.md` with `type: generated`, `generator: conversation-digest`, `gen_kind: report`, `iso_week`, `period_start`, `period_end`, `review_state: raw`.
3. Sections, in this order: a coverage line first (prompts in `history.jsonl` against prompts with event rows, per day, with the gap named); counts per rule flag with prompt ids; the five prompts that started with the largest context; the ten largest tool results; files read twice; tool errors per tool, with interrupts counted apart from errors; skills, subagents, and MCP tools used and, from the installed lists, never used; instruction files loaded; compactions and permission denials; the Claude Code versions seen; then the review sample: about 10 random prompts (seed printed) and up to 15 flagged, as ids and links. This replaces the manual sample rules written in P4.
4. Tests: a synthetic events file with known flags and a golden digest; idempotence (two runs, identical bytes); a file with missing fields; an empty week (the digest says so plainly, which is a valid result); a week with a coverage gap (the gap is the first line). Use `node --test` or Pester, whichever the script language needs.
5. Validate against reality: run it on the days of real events available. The owner hand-counts ten sampled prompts (tool calls, errors, files read) from the session notes and compares with the digest. Provide the ten and the digest values side by side, and do not mark them equal yourself.
6. Time the run on a week of events. It must finish in under 30 seconds (a working ceiling).
7. Patch [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Weekly Review Protocol|Weekly Review Protocol]] step 1 and 2 with a dated entry pointing at the digest path, and [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Scripts|Capture Scripts]] with a card for the script.
## Report Back
- The flag definitions and thresholds as written.
- Test output, the run time, and one real digest's path.
- The side-by-side hand-count table for the owner, with the owner's verdict if they gave one.
- What the digest could not compute (fields the events do not carry) and what you propose.
## What Not To Do
- Do not put prompt text, assistant text, or pasted content in the digest.
- Do not score or rank sessions. The digest counts; it does not judge.
- Do not call a model to word the digest.
## Related Notes
[[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Per-Prompt Record|Per-Prompt Record]], [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Weekly Review Protocol|Weekly Review Protocol]], [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Failure Taxonomy|Failure Taxonomy]].

# P7 - Schedule and Review Nudge
Paste into a fresh Claude Code session started in the Jarvis vault: `Read and run 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P7 - Schedule and Review Nudge.md. Follow Hard Rules and Report Back exactly.`
## Why This Exists
A loop that depends on remembering to run a script is the loop that died in week three before. The owner asked for scheduled runs. This prompt makes the digest generate itself, creates the week's review skeleton, and puts "review due" in front of the owner on `/startday`. The review itself stays human. It is new in the revised order; the original [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Loop Build Plan|Build Plan]] left scheduling as "by hand or by schedule".
- **Verified 2026-10-22:** `Jarvis-WeeklyReview` runs Sundays 06:00, last ran 2026-10-18 with result 0, and it runs the concept-mastery `/weekly-review`, not a conversation review. The pattern for a task is `register-weekly-review-task.ps1` plus `weekly-review-silent.vbs` plus `run-weekly-review.ps1` with a lock file and a log. `check-syncthing-status.ps1` holds the proven toast code. `ConversationCapture-Backfill-Windows` registers only on the Acer and runs dry unless `-Apply`.
- **Verified in the standards:** [[30_Order/Standards/AI Loop/Review System|Review System]] says "nothing runs on its own", meaning a review is triggered by the owner. A generator that builds the input to a review is not a review; P3 records the wording conflict, and Review System is not edited until build B10.
- **Unknown, step 1 checks:** whether the Unison task (`SecondBrainClaudekit-JarvisSync`, not registered on the Acer) would overwrite `.claude/skills` edits if it were registered here.
- **Owner decision 2026-10-22:** skills and hooks live in the Jarvis `.claude/` only.
## Hard Rules
- Scope is the Acer, Windows side. The scheduled task makes no model call. Do not change `Jarvis-WeeklyReview`, `Jarvis-GitAutoSync`, or the backfill task.
- Do not edit `30_Order/Standards`, `Templates`, or `Workflows`, or `00_Dashboard.md`.
- Never read or print `.credentials.json`, `.env*`, tokens, or `history.jsonl` text.
- The register script is a dry run unless `-Apply`, refuses any host except `GUPTA-BUILDS`, and logs to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\claude-capture\`.
- The runner takes a lock, writes `logs/conversation-digest.log` (add ignore lines for it as P5 did, proven with `git check-ignore -v`), and exits with an honest code.
- Create the weekly review skeleton only if it does not exist. Never overwrite an owner-edited note.
- Do not run `git commit`. After any vault write, look for `.sync-conflict-*` copies.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, patch by heading, update `updated:`.
- Do not set the status of this prompt to `complete`.
## Implementation Order
1. Read `register-weekly-review-task.ps1`, `weekly-review-silent.vbs`, `run-weekly-review.ps1`, `register-capture-backfill-windows-task.ps1`, and `register-jarvis-sync-task.ps1` with `sync-jarvis-silent.vbs`. Confirm whether Unison could overwrite `.claude/skills`. If it could, stop and ask the owner before step 6.
2. Write `scripts/run-conversation-digest.ps1`: compute the ISO week (default: the current week, run on Sunday evening; a `-Week` parameter for any other), call the P6 digest script, then create `AI Tools Weekly Review - <ISO week>.md` in `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/Weekly/` from the AI Weekly Review Template with the Templater placeholders rendered by the script (date, title), `status: seed`, Period Covered filled, and a link to the digest. Exit non-zero if the digest fails.
3. Write `scripts/conversation-digest-silent.vbs` and `scripts/register-conversation-digest-task.ps1` modeled on the weekly-review pair: Sundays 21:00 by default (a parameter), hidden, start when available, 10-minute limit, ignore new instances, runs on battery.
4. Toast on completion using the `check-syncthing-status.ps1` pattern: the week, prompts found, coverage percentage, and "review due". Toast on failure as well.
5. Test: run the runner by hand for a past week; run it twice and confirm the second run does not overwrite the skeleton; register with `-Apply`; run `Start-ScheduledTask`; read `LastTaskResult` (must be 0), the log line, and the new files.
6. Nudge: in `.claude/skills/starting-day/SKILL.md` (confirm the path first), add one instruction to `/startday`: if the newest `AI Tools Weekly Review` note has `status: seed` and today is Sunday through Tuesday, add "Do the weekly conversation review (about 60 minutes the first four weeks)" to Today's Focus. Patch by heading and keep the skill under its length limit.
7. Add the task to [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]] and the trigger table in `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/README.md`, each with a dated entry.
## Report Back
- What the Unison check found.
- The registered task's schedule, `LastTaskResult`, and the log line from the test run.
- The skeleton note's path, and proof that a second run left it untouched.
- The `/startday` diff and the toast text.
- Anything the owner must decide, such as a different day or time.
## What Not To Do
- Do not let the scheduled task write codes, flags, or findings. It only prepares the review.
- Do not auto-open the review note or change the owner's dashboard.
- Do not register this on the Dell.

# P8 - Capture Watchdog
Paste into a fresh Claude Code session started in the Jarvis vault: `Read and run 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P8 - Capture Watchdog.md. Follow Hard Rules and Report Back exactly.`
## Why This Exists
Capture has failed silently at least five times and a person noticed every time ([[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Layer Postmortem|postmortem]] finding 2). `00 - Capture Health.md` records an exit code, which stays 0 while a hook points at a missing folder. No file lists what should be registered ([[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]]). This is build B8, the dead-man's switch: a check on its own schedule that looks for the absence of evidence, as in the healthchecks pattern the 2026-10-08 session cited.
- **Verified 2026-10-22:** the backfill task runs every 30 minutes with result 0; `Jarvis-Syncthing-Health` is the only existing alarm and uses a toast; the activity log and the health state files live outside synced paths.
- **Depends on:** P2 (the `export_result` lines), P5 (event rows and the settings entries), P7 (the digest task).
- **Design rule, from the 10-08 session:** the watcher must not be the thing it watches, and the grace window is 1.5 times the expected cadence.
## Hard Rules
- Scope is the Acer, Windows side. The watchdog is read-only on everything except its own state and log. It never repairs and never restarts a task.
- Do not edit `30_Order/Standards`, `Templates`, or `Workflows`, or `00_Dashboard.md`.
- Never read or print `.credentials.json`, `.env*`, tokens, or `history.jsonl` text. Mtimes and counts only.
- Compatible with Windows PowerShell 5.1. All paths come from parameters with defaults, so tests can point at synthetic folders.
- Toast only on a change of state into WARN or FAIL, and once when it clears. No repeats.
- Do not run `git commit`; add ignore lines for its state and log as in P5 and prove them. Look for `.sync-conflict-*` after writing.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, patch by heading, update `updated:`.
- Do not set the status of this prompt to `complete`.
## Implementation Order
1. Write `capture-expected.json` in `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/`: what must be true on this machine (hook entries and events, `cleanupPeriodDays` at or above 45, scheduled tasks and their cadence, the tracked project list). It is versioned in git and is the answer to "no file says what should be registered".
2. Write `scripts/check-conversation-capture.ps1`. Each check returns OK, WARN, or FAIL with its evidence: (1) settings match the expected hooks and retention; (2) each expected task exists, is enabled, last result 0, last run younger than 1.5 times its cadence; (3) if a tracked-project transcript changed in the last N hours, event rows exist in that window; (4) the newest note is not older than its transcript by more than 35 minutes; (5) the oldest transcript is younger than `cleanupPeriodDays`; (6) `.sync-conflict-*` count under the capture and Conversations folders; (7) the digest task's last result; (8) informational: the stray statusline temp file count.
3. Write state to `logs/capture-watchdog-<machine>.json` (last result per check, last state change) and a toast on state change. Exit 0 for OK, 1 for WARN, 2 for FAIL.
4. Register it with its own task, every 60 minutes, using the same register-script pattern as P7 (dry run unless `-Apply`, host-locked). It is not called from any other script.
5. Tests with synthetic folders: each check passing and failing; the debounce (a second run in the same state sends no second toast).
6. Kill drill, written for the owner: set `JARVIS_EVENTS_OFF=1` as a user environment variable, use Claude Code in a tracked project for a few minutes, wait past the grace window or run the watchdog with `-GraceMinutes 5`, confirm a FAIL naming the events gap and a toast, then unset the variable and confirm it clears. Repeat once by disabling the backfill task for an hour. Do not run the drills yourself in a way that disables the owner's real capture without telling them first.
7. Patch [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]] (it now points at `capture-expected.json`) and [[60_Claude/07_AI_Information/How to Use Claude/Track Conversations|Track Conversations]] (the manual checks become "run the watchdog, then read these"), each with a dated entry.
## Report Back
- The expected-state file and the first live run's table of eight checks.
- Test output and the task registration result.
- The kill drill result (or the drill instructions if the owner has not run it), with the time between the break and the alert.
- Any check that produced a false alarm on live data.
## What Not To Do
- Do not call this from the hook or the backfill. A dead job cannot report its own death.
- Do not alert on every WARN run.
- Do not fold in Dell, WSL, Cursor, or Cowork checks.

# P9 - Loop Closed Rehearsal
Paste into a fresh Claude Code session started in the Jarvis vault: `Read and run 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P9 - Loop Closed Rehearsal.md. Follow Hard Rules and Report Back exactly.`
## Why This Exists
The owner's finish line for the Claude Code loop is: scheduled runs work, a test run passes, the human review loop has run, and existing sessions are captured on the Acer. The original build plan had a check per build and no check for the whole. This prompt is that check, run once on a real week. Nothing in it builds anything.
- **Cannot start before:** P0 to P8 are complete and reviewed, and at least seven days of event rows exist from P5. If not, say so and stop.
- **Honest limit:** the loop is not closed until a change made in a weekly review has been judged against its metric on its review date. This prompt can declare the loop running; it can only declare it closed when the first ledger row has been reviewed (usually two weeks after the first review).
## Hard Rules
- Scope is the Acer, Windows side, tracked projects.
- Do not fix anything you find. Every failed check becomes a finding and, if needed, a build-plan row. The only writes are the report note, the hub Log and Current State, and the Build Plan row status.
- Do not edit `30_Order/Standards`, `Templates`, or `Workflows`.
- Never read or print `.credentials.json`, `.env*`, tokens, `history.jsonl` text, or transcript text.
- Do not run `git commit`; look for `.sync-conflict-*` after writing.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, patch by heading, update `updated:`.
- Do not set the status of this prompt to `complete`.
## Implementation Order
1. Check the precondition above and list the P-prompts with their statuses.
2. Run the twelve checks. For each one write the command or file you used, the output, and pass or fail.
   1. **Coverage.** For the last seven days and the tracked projects, prompts in `history.jsonl` (count only) against prompts with event rows. Pass at 100%, or every gap explained by a known cause (a slash command, a headless run).
   2. **Unattended schedule.** The digest task ran without anyone starting it: task result 0, a log line, a digest file for the week, and the review skeleton.
   3. **Digest accuracy.** Ten sampled prompts hand-counted by the owner match the digest.
   4. **Human pass.** The owner's blind pass took 60 minutes or less, and the ledger holds a row with change, metric, baseline, and a review date.
   5. **Fresh-session test.** The owner repeated the prompt that failed in a fresh session after the change, and wrote down the result.
   6. **Kill drill.** The watchdog turned FAIL within its grace window after capture was disabled, and cleared after it was re-enabled.
   7. **Retention.** The oldest transcript is older than 30 days and still on disk.
   8. **Sync.** No `.sync-conflict-*` under the capture or Conversations folders; the latest Auto-sync commit contains the digest and does not contain any `events-*.jsonl`.
   9. **Privacy.** A pattern search of the events file and the digest for the exporter's secret prefixes finds nothing, and the digest holds no prompt text.
   10. **Overhead.** The measured p95 per event from P5 still holds on the week's data, and the owner reports no slower sessions.
   11. **Back-out.** Setting `JARVIS_EVENTS_OFF=1` stops event writes within one prompt, and removing the hook entries returns Claude Code to its old behavior. Both steps are written in `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/README.md`.
   12. **Docs match reality.** [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]], [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Scripts|Capture Scripts]], and the Build Plan describe what is installed, with no stale "not built" lines.
3. Write `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Conversations/Loop Closed Rehearsal 2026-W<N>.md` as an audit (`review_kind: audit`) with the twelve results.
4. Update the hub's Current State and Log, and mark each Build Plan row. If every check passes, write "loop running, not yet closed: first ledger review due <date>". If any fails, write the failing check first.
5. Queue the next prompts only after this one, because their inputs now exist: B6 local reviewer pilot (needs 30 hand-coded prompts), B7 `/insights` skill (Jarvis `.claude/`), B9 Dell rollout (repo conversations only: Jarvis, internship-research-loop, second-brain-claudekit, portfolio, CausalOps, tradingview, re-exported from live transcripts), B10 rewrite of the `30_Order` conversation standards, then Codex. List what each one needs as input; do not write them.
## Report Back
- The twelve-row result table, with evidence.
- Every finding, ranked, and what each blocks.
- The state of each open owner decision.
- The exact date the first ledger row is due for review.
## What Not To Do
- Do not mark a check passed on a count of runs. Mark it on evidence from this week.
- Do not declare the loop closed.
- Do not repair a failing check and then re-run it as if nothing happened.

*2026-10-22, later the same day. The sections above are kept as written.*
### What had changed since the plan was written
- B1 is half done. A Dell session on 2026-10-22 added message-id token dedup and vault-root-aware project routing to both exporters, with `test-token-dedup.ps1` and `test-project-routing.ps1`. The dedup test passes under PowerShell 7 (re-run on the Acer). The price table, `status: seed`, the BOM, and the fenced commands are still open. The changes are uncommitted in the shared tree.
- The Dell's `Jarvis-GitAutoSync` is disabled. The Acer is the only git writer, so the Syncthing conflict risk in the handoff is much smaller (0 live conflict copies found).
- The oldest transcript on the Acer is dated 2026-09-24 and moves forward daily, so B0 is urgent, not just first.
- Claude Code is 2.1.309 on the Acer, not 2.1.307.
- The events folder is not "already ignored by git and Syncthing". Only named log files are listed, so P5 adds the ignore lines.
- The weekly note's home in [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Weekly Review Protocol|Weekly Review Protocol]] conflicts with the Note Type Registry. The registry wins: `60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/Weekly/`, using the AI Weekly Review Template. P4 settles the mapping.
- "One commit per build" is replaced by one session log entry per build, because `Jarvis-GitAutoSync` commits every 30 minutes and is the only git writer.
### Owner decisions, 2026-10-22
| Decision | Answer |
|---|---|
| `cleanupPeriodDays` on the Acer | 60 days. Never 0. |
| Dell scope | Repo conversations only: Jarvis, internship-research-loop, second-brain-claudekit, portfolio, CausalOps, tradingview. The rest of the Dell archive is clutter and out of scope. Build B9. |
| Acer tree | Fresh tree and the test bed for the new design. Project-based sessions are re-exported from live transcripts. Older notes whose transcripts were swept stay, recognizable by having no `review_state`. |
| Weekly reviewer | The person reads and codes first, the local model flags afterward. |
| Skill and hook home | The Jarvis vault `.claude/` only. |
| Events | Per machine, and scoped to tracked projects so The Plan's personal content stays out of Jarvis logs. |

## B versus P

- **B (B0 to B10)** was the build list from the earlier research session.
- **P (P0, P1, …)** is my regrouping of that list into one session prompt per phase, plus phases it was missing.
- **From now on use P only.** The mapping is in the Build Plan, under Corrections ("Numbering: B and P").

## The prompt files

I read the Sonnet 5.5 guide and the general best-practices page before rewriting.

- **Format.** Each file has a run-by line, a guide link, a suggested effort level, then the whole prompt in one fenced block you can copy. P0 and P1 are shown as the model to follow. I added a short "Run Record" under each so you can note when it ran and your review.
- **Guide techniques used:**
  - XML-tagged sections.
  - Verified facts labelled apart from assumptions.
  - The guide's wording on scope, carrying on to the end, and real verification.
  - A search step where prices may have changed.
  - An instruction for progress updates.
- **Fully written:** P0, P1, P2. They cite the real exporter code and today's machine state.
- **Detailed but refreshed by what P0 to P2 return:** P3 hook timing, P4 design postmortem (Opus 5.5), P5 review notes and your first hand review.
- **Drafts, marked "do not run until…":** P6 event hook, P7 weekly digest, P8 schedules and review nudge.

I deleted my nine earlier drafts, which all had the old numbering.

## Mistakes the first three prompts now guard against

- **Time limit on P0.** The oldest transcript turns 30 days old at 2026-10-24 17:56, and the next session start after that deletes it. P0 states this deadline.
- **Junction hazard in P2.** Each project folder contains `_raw_jsonl`, a junction pointing at your live transcripts. A recursive delete on such a folder can destroy them. P2 removes the link with `cmd /c rmdir`, checks the transcript count before and after each step, and archives nothing outside the vault until you approve a dry-run plan.
- **Stickiness blocks new routing.** The exporter keeps a session in the folder where its marker already sits. Adding routing rules alone would move nothing, so P2 includes a controlled reroute.
- **BOM fix could create duplicates.** Under Windows PowerShell 5.1, removing the byte-order mark without reading markers as UTF-8 would give titles like "weeks 1–4" a second note. P1 fixes the reads together with the writes and tests it.

## Your decisions

- **Project folders:** Jarvis, The Plan, Home, UMN Courses (everything under `D:\_Anant\10_Areas\UMN`), and any other directory gets its own folder. The rules live in one editable file, `capture-projects.json`. I dropped my earlier idea of excluding The Plan, since events stay local and the synced digest carries ids only.
- **Retention:** 60 days on the Acer; the Dell later, repos only.
- **Schedule:** digest on Sundays at 21:00 while testing. I added a Schedule section to `Capture Registration.md` listing every task on the Acer with its real trigger times. New tasks must take a free minute.
- **Completion:** the Build Plan now defines "complete" in eight conditions, each tied to a phase. It says the loop is closed only once the first Intervention Ledger row has been judged on its review date.
- **Other platforms and the Dell:** P12 documents where Codex, Cursor and other sessions live and how to clean up the Dell's existing material. It builds nothing.

## Assumptions to confirm

- **Scratch folder (my choice):** Claude's own scratch folders under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\claude` would go to a `Scratch` folder, so test runs like `denytest` don't each create a project. P2 asks you to confirm.
- **"UMN courses" as one folder:** all course work lands in a single `UMN Courses` folder, not one per course.
- **P4 runs on Opus 5.5:** I ran the Sonnet guide on it and noted that the Opus 5.5 page may differ. I did not read that page.

# Vault Lint
## Purpose
Measures how many notes fit their Standard and checks that the kit files (Standards, Templates, Workflows) agree with each other. Without it the goal of 90% fit has no number, and a Standard can drift from its Template unnoticed.
## Trigger
Run by hand: `node vault-lint.js` from this folder. The weekly review records the fit percentage. Run `--kit` after any change to a Standard, Template, or Workflow. No scheduled task exists yet.
## Inputs and Outputs
Reads every `.md` file under the vault root (found three folders up from the script), the Spec blocks in `30_Order/Standards/`, the vocabulary tables in [[Frontmatter Standard]], and `vault-lint.config.json`. It writes nothing into the vault. `--json FILE` writes a report wherever you point it, whic
```powershell
node vault-lint.js                                  # fit %, by tier, by family, top failure reasons
node vault-lint.js --path 30_Order/Standards/       # one folder
node vault-lint.js --family course-concept --list 20   # failing notes in a family
node vault-lint.js --specs                          # Spec blocks, names, empty files
node vault-lint.js --kit                            # Standard, Template, Workflow consistency
```
## State
None. `vault-lint.config.json` is tracked source: `out_of_scope`, `tier4`, and `template_paths` regular expressions. The type and status vocabulary is read from the tables in [[Frontmatter Standard]]; change the tables, not the script.
## Failure Behavior
It never edits a note. A Spec block that is not valid JSON is reported as an error, and notes of that family then fall to `unmapped`. `--kit` exits with status 1 when it finds an error, so it can gate a commit.
## Verify
Run `node vault-lint.js --specs` and compare the Spec count with the number of Spec blocks you wrote. Lint a note you know fits and one you know does not. The scanned count plus the excluded counts equals the number of `.md` files under the root.
## Retire
Delete this folder in one commit and remove the lint step from the weekly review. The fit number then goes back to being unmeasured.
```

## Prompt - P0 Retention and Transcript Snapshot
````
<role>
You are making one configuration change to Claude Code on the owner's Acer laptop and taking a safety copy of the session transcripts that Claude Code would otherwise delete. You verify with real commands and report plainly.
</role>

<context>
The owner is building a loop that reviews their own Claude Code sessions every week. The loop reads the raw transcripts in C:\Users\anant\.claude\projects. Claude Code deletes those after 30 days by default, so each day that passes removes the oldest day of history. This task stops that loss for the Acer (Windows side) and keeps a copy of what exists now. The vault's CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md load automatically; this prompt adds only what they do not cover.

Facts verified on 2026-10-22 between 23:00 and 23:45 local. Re-check each in step 1 and report any that differ, because the machine may have changed since.
- Machine GUPTA-BUILDS, Claude Code 2.1.309, Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 both installed.
- C:\Users\anant\.claude\settings.json has 83 lines and these top-level keys: permissions, hooks, statusLine, enabledPlugins, autoUpdatesChannel, theme, autoCompactEnabled, switchModelsOnFlag, autoContinueAtUsageLimit, autoMode. It has no cleanupPeriodDays key and no env block. The top-level "hooks": { sits at line 17.
- 124 top-level transcripts (*.jsonl directly inside a project folder). Oldest last-written 2026-09-24 17:56, newest 2026-10-22 23:42. Per project folder: C--Users-anant 25, [REDACTED] 71, [REDACTED] 25, and three folders with one each (the 4061 course folder, the 4521 Code folder, and a denytest scratch folder).
- C:\Users\anant\.claude\.last-cleanup held 2026-10-23T03:57Z, which matches the start of a session. It held 2026-10-22T01:53Z earlier the same day. So the sweep runs at session start (inferred from those two readings, not documented).
- C:\Users\anant\.claude\projects is 585 MB. C: has 171.5 GB free and D: has 510.2 GB free.
- D:\_Anant\99_Archive exists. It is outside the vault, so nothing placed there is synced or committed.
- From the Claude Code docs (read 2026-10-21 and 22): cleanupPeriodDays defaults to 30 and its minimum is 1. The sweep also removes subagent files, tool-results, file-history, plans, paste-cache and usage-data (the /insights reports) in the same period. It leaves history.jsonl alone.
- Conflicting evidence on the value 0: the docs say it fails validation, and a public GitHub issue says it stopped transcripts from being written at all. Treat 0 as unsafe.

Owner decision, 2026-10-22: the Acer keeps 60 days. That covers the whole working life of this laptop, whose first notes date from 2026-09-17. The Dell is handled in a later phase and is out of scope here.
</context>

<goal>
The setting "cleanupPeriodDays": 60 is in C:\Users\anant\.claude\settings.json, and nothing else in that file changed. A copy of every live transcript exists in D:\_Anant\99_Archive\Claude Transcript Snapshot 2026-10-22 with a manifest. The vault records both facts for later phases. The result is verified by reading the files back, except the sweep itself, which can only be checked later and is reported as pending.
</goal>

<constraints>
- Touch only these four things: settings.json (one added key), the snapshot folder under D:\_Anant\99_Archive, one new row in the vault note 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration.md, and one new line in the Log of 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Layer.md. Every other change would be hard to attribute later.
- Edit settings.json with one targeted Edit. Parsing the file and writing it back can reorder or drop keys, so do not do that.
- Delete nothing under C:\Users\anant\.claude, including the stray .statusline-ratelimit-cache.json.tmp.* files. Report them only.
- Read settings.json with the Read tool. It holds permission rules, hooks and flags, no credentials. If any value in it ever looks like a credential, do not repeat it.
- Never open or print .credentials.json, any .env file, history.jsonl, or the text of a transcript. Counting files and bytes is enough here.
- Do not run git commit or git push. Jarvis-GitAutoSync commits the vault every 30 minutes and is the only git writer.
- Do not use the value 0 or any value below 45 for cleanupPeriodDays.
- Write vault notes in plain sentences with no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, and path-qualified wikilinks. Patch by heading and set updated: to today's date.
- When the work the user asked for is done and checked, stop and report. Don't add features, tests, files, docs or refactors that weren't asked for. If you think one would help, mention it at the end instead of doing it.
- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step.
</constraints>

## Prompt - P1 Exporter Correctness
````
<role>
You are a careful PowerShell engineer fixing the Windows Claude Code session exporter in the owner's Jarvis vault. Every behavior you change gets a test that fails on the old code and passes on the new.
</role>

<context>
The exporter turns each Claude Code session transcript into one Markdown note under 60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Acer/Windows/Claude/<project>/. It is 921 lines at 30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/export-claude-session.ps1. The hook jarvis-session-continuity.ps1 runs it on every Stop and SessionEnd under Windows PowerShell 5.1 (the command is "powershell", not "pwsh"). A scheduled task runs it with -BackfillAll every 30 minutes. The owner will review their own sessions every week from these notes, so the notes must be correct, readable and conform to the vault's Standard. Read the code before you describe or change it; line numbers below were read on 2026-10-22 and drift, so find each place by name.

What is already fixed. A Dell session on 2026-10-22 added deduplication of token usage by message id (Set-ResponseUsage, Add-DedupedModelUsage), a vault-root-aware Get-ProjectName, and Find-ExistingSessionDir (a session keeps the folder where its .exported marker already lives). It added test-token-dedup.ps1 and test-project-routing.ps1 beside the exporter. Jarvis-GitAutoSync committed all of it by 23:33; git status was clean afterward (verify with git log -3 on the exporter file). Do not redo this work.

What is still wrong, by reading the code:
- Pricing table (Pricing hashtable near lines 36 to 46; its comment near lines 29 to 35 says Sonnet 5's introductory rate ends 2026-08-31). It has no entry for claude-opus-5-5, claude-sonnet-5-5 or claude-haiku-5-5, so cost_usd is written as null for those models: 14, 52 and 7 old notes. The cost formula (near lines 691 to 694) multiplies the input rate by 0.1 for cache reads, 1.25 for 5-minute cache writes and 2.0 for 1-hour cache writes.
- Frontmatter (near lines 728 to 772) writes status: raw, which the vault's Frontmatter Standard bans. It writes no review_state, created or updated. The Standard for this note type is 30_Order/Standards/AI Loop/Conversations.md (spec "ai-conversation-export"): status must be seed, review_state must be raw, created and updated must be dates. Allowed review_state values are raw, unreviewed, reviewed.
- Encoding: notes, markers and the two rollup files are written with Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 (near lines 341, 373, 823, 827). Under Windows PowerShell 5.1 that adds a byte-order mark; 351 of 630 old notes start with one. The Standard wants UTF-8 without a BOM.
- A related hazard: the marker holds the note's file name, and the exporter reads it back with Get-Content -Raw (near line 448) and reads note heads with Get-Content -TotalCount 20 (near line 458), both without -Encoding UTF8. Under 5.1, a BOM-less UTF-8 file read without an encoding is decoded as ANSI, so a title containing a character such as the en dash in "weeks 1–4" would not match its own file name on the next export and would create a second note. Fixing the BOM without fixing these reads would introduce that bug.
- Rendering (Format-ToolCall near lines 127 to 178): Bash commands are written as one inline backtick span, even when the command spans several lines, and PowerShell, Agent, Skill and MCP calls fall into a default branch that writes an inline JSON dump. Obsidian's Tasks plugin parses the span's inner lines as task syntax and warns hundreds of times on one note. Fenced blocks use a fixed three-backtick fence, so tool output that itself contains three backticks closes the fence early. Edit diffs prefix only the first line of a multi-line old_string and new_string with - and +.
- Update-Rollups (near lines 317 to 374) writes 00 - Session Index.md and 00 - Tool Usage Rollup.md with type: index. The Standard wants type: generated, a generator key and gen_kind: index (30_Order/Standards/Generated Output Standard.md).
- Tests: scripts/test-token-dedup.ps1 (passes under PowerShell 7 as of 2026-10-22; not yet run under 5.1), scripts/test-project-routing.ps1, and an older scripts/test-token-aggregation.ps1 that nothing runs.

Vault conventions that matter here. Wikilinks in notes are path-qualified. Notes use no em dashes. The .ps1 files that contain non-ASCII characters carry a byte-order mark on purpose, because 5.1 misreads them without one; keep any BOM that is already on a .ps1 and add no non-ASCII character to a script.
</context>

<goal>
Notes written by the exporter conform to the Conversations Standard, carry correct token totals, a cost for every model that Claude Code has used on this machine (or null with a stated reason), no BOM, and tool calls rendered so that Obsidian's Tasks plugin raises no warning. All test scripts pass under both shells, through a single runner. No existing note is touched.
</goal>

<constraints>

The WSL exporter (wsl-session-export.ps1) stays as it is except that its existing test must stay green; note its gaps in your report. The WSL side is outside this phase.
- Write nothing into the vault's conversation folders. Real transcripts may be read by the exporter and by a read-only script, but all output goes to the session scratchpad or the temp folder. Existing notes must keep their bytes exactly.
- Never print or copy the text of a real transcript into your report. Use synthetic transcripts in tests. For real-data checks, report numbers only.

In the frontmatter block write status: seed, then review_state: raw, then created and updated. created is the date part of started_at, and updated is the date part of exported_at; if started_at is empty use the exported date. Add no other key. Keep every existing key and its order where possible.
4. Encoding. Add one helper that writes text as UTF-8 without a BOM using [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText and New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false). Use it for notes, markers and both rollup files. Make every read of a marker or a note head use -Encoding UTF8 explicitly.

Use them everywhere the exporter writes code. Render Bash and PowerShell commands as fenced blocks tagged bash and powershell. Render a PowerShell call's result the way Bash results already are. Render the default branch's JSON dump with Format-InlineCode. Prefix every line of a multi-line old_string and new_string in Edit and MultiEdit diffs with - and +. Keep Read, Write, Grep and Glob lines recognizable.
7. Test override. Let the environment variable JARVIS_CAPTURE_OUTROOT replace the computed OutRoot when it is set. Document it in the script header as test-only. This lets you export real transcripts to the scratchpad without writing into the vault.

It asserts: status is seed and review_state is raw; created and updated are valid dates; the first three bytes of a note, a marker and a rollup are not EF BB BF; a multi-line Bash command and a PowerShell command are fenced and no line inside any inline span begins with - or a digit and a period; tool output containing three backticks stays inside a longer fence; a multi-line Edit diff has the prefix on every line; the three newly priced models give a numeric cost and an unknown model gives null; rollups are type: generated with gen_kind: index; and an en dash in the title survives a second export of the same session with no second note created (touch the transcript so it is newer than the marker). The last four folders named before denytest, plus Conversations, hold sessions whose cwd is a subfolder of the Jarvis vault: Conversations, Jesse, Plans and sync-workflow are all inside the vault.
- Get-ProjectName (near lines 61 to 75 of scripts/export-claude-session.ps1) knows only the vault, The Plan and Home. Find-ExistingSessionDir (near lines 81 to 95) keeps a session in whichever folder already holds its .exported marker. So adding rules alone will not move any existing note; the move is this phase's job.

Remove-Item -Recurse on a folder that contains the junction can delete the real transcripts on some Windows PowerShell versions. Only cmd /c rmdir "<junction path>" removes the link alone. Those transcripts are the data this whole project exists to protect.
- P0 saved a copy of the live transcripts to D:\_Anant\99_Archive\Claude Transcript Snapshot 2026-10-22 and set cleanupPeriodDays to 60. P1 finished the note format (status seed, review_state raw, created and updated, no BOM, fenced commands, deduplicated tokens, priced models) and added scripts/run-all-tests.ps1 and the JARVIS_CAPTURE_OUTROOT test override. Each rule has a project name, a match kind (under or exact), and a path with tokens: {vault}, {root} (the folder three levels above the vault, which is D:\_Anant), {home} (USERPROFILE) and {localappdata}. Rules, in order: Jarvis under {vault}; The Plan under the sibling folder "The Plan" of the vault's parent; UMN Courses under {root}\10_Areas\UMN; Home exact {home}; Scratch under {localappdata}\Temp\claude. Run under both shells.
6. Coverage script. Write scripts/check-capture-coverage.ps1, read-only, with parameters for the transcripts root, the notes root and the routing file so tests can use synthetic folders. For every top-level transcript it reports: session id, whether it has any assistant turn (use a fast text search for the assistant type marker; do not parse whole files), whether exactly one note exists for that id in the notes root (match the session_id line in the note head), and whether that note sits in the folder the routing rules give for the transcript's first cwd. 7. Dry-run plan. Write scripts/reroute-acer-notes.ps1, dry-run by default, apply only with -Apply. For every note in the Acer tree it computes the target folder from the note's cwd frontmatter and the rules. It plans: (a) moves for notes in the wrong folder with no live transcript (the file, its .exported marker, and the project: line in the note's frontmatter updated to the target name); (b) for sessions with a live transcript, no move at all, because step 10 regenerates them in the right folder and the old note is archived after the new one is verified; (c) for a session with two notes, keeping the one with the later exported_at and archiving the other; (d) retirement of any folder that ends up with no notes. ## Prompt - P3 Hook Timing and Failure Record
````
<role>
You are measuring and hardening the Claude Code Stop and SessionEnd hook on the owner's Acer laptop so that a slow or failing export is visible and cannot slow the owner down.
</role>

<context>
The hook 30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/jarvis-session-continuity.ps1 runs the session exporter on every Stop (after every assistant turn) and on SessionEnd, in the foreground, under Windows PowerShell 5.1. It appends one line to C:\Users\anant\.claude\jarvis-session-activity.jsonl before the export, runs the exporter with its output discarded, and swallows every exception in empty catch blocks. Result: nobody knows how long the export takes, and a failed export leaves no trace. Five silent capture failures happened between July and October 2026 and a person noticed each one.

Documented behavior, re-read from the Claude Code hooks reference on 2026-10-22:
- Hooks block Claude until they finish unless the command hook sets "async": true. An async hook runs in the background and its "timeout" is not enforced.
- SessionEnd hooks have a default timeout of 1.5 seconds. A per-hook "timeout" (up to 60 seconds) or the environment variable CLAUDE_CODE_SESSIONEND_HOOKS_TIMEOUT_MS raises it.
- The docs do not say whether an async hook survives SessionEnd in an interactive session. In headless runs (claude -p) any async hook still running at teardown is killed.
- transcript_path "may lag the in-memory conversation", so a Stop-time export can miss the newest messages. Stop provides last_assistant_message for the final text.
- Matching hooks run in parallel; an identical handler registered in more than one settings file runs once.

Verified by reading files on 2026-10-22: C:\Users\anant\.claude\settings.json registers Stop and SessionEnd at lines 18 to 38, both running powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "D:/_Anant/20_Progress/Documents/Jarvis/30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/jarvis-session-continuity.ps1", with no timeout and no async. The exporter prints "Status: <status> -- Project: <name>" to stdout in single-session mode, and Export-Session returns a status object (dup, junk, written, error, legacy). The exporter is 921 lines; the largest transcript on this machine is over 8 MB. After P2 the exporter also reads a routing file on every call.

Assumed, to verify: a full re-render of a long session on every Stop is the slow case; the "dup" early return is fast.
</context>

<goal>
A measured table of how long the hook takes on small, median and largest transcripts for Stop and SessionEnd; a recorded outcome line for every export in the activity log, written before any error is swallowed; and hook settings chosen from the measurements, with the reasoning stated. Nothing about what the exporter renders changes.
</goal>

<constraints>
- Edit only: the hook script, the exporter's header and path handling for two test overrides, the two Stop and SessionEnd entries of C:\Users\anant\.claude\settings.json (targeted edits, after copying the file to the scratchpad), a new scripts/test-session-hook.ps1, and the notes named in step 8.
- Do not let measurement write into the vault or the real activity log. Use JARVIS_CAPTURE_OUTROOT (from P1) to send note output to the scratchpad, and add a test-only JARVIS_ACTIVITY_LOG override for the activity file.
- Never open .credentials.json, .env files, history.jsonl, or print transcript text. Pointing the hook at a real transcript path is fine; report milliseconds only.
- The hook must exit 0 on every input and must never throw from a log write. Keep it compatible with Windows PowerShell 5.1 and keep its BOM; add no non-ASCII character.
- Do not run git commit or git push; Jarvis-GitAutoSync is the only git writer.
- Test under pwsh and powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File.
- When the work the user asked for is done and checked, stop and report. Don't add features, tests, files, docs or refactors that weren't asked for. If you think one would help, mention it at the end instead of doing it.
- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step.
</constraints>

<progress_updates>
Before your first tool call, say in one sentence what you are about to do. After each numbered step, write one line with the result. End with a short recap.
</progress_updates>

<steps>
1. Add the two test overrides (JARVIS_ACTIVITY_LOG in the hook, and confirm JARVIS_CAPTURE_OUTROOT from P1 reaches the exporter when the hook calls it). Document both as test-only.
2. Measure with Measure-Command, five runs per cell, in a fresh powershell.exe -NoProfile process piping synthetic JSON to the hook: first an empty script to get bare PowerShell startup; then the hook with garbage stdin; then the hook for event Stop and for SessionEnd with transcript_path pointing at the smallest non-empty, the median and the largest top-level transcript under C:\Users\anant\.claude\projects, each with a unique fake session_id so the export really runs; then once more with the same session_id so the "already current" early return is measured. Print a table of p50 and p95 in milliseconds. Subtract nothing; report raw numbers and the startup baseline beside them.
3. Add the outcome record. Capture the exporter's returned status and the elapsed milliseconds, and append a second line to the activity file: {timestamp, event: "export_result", session_id, status, ms, error}. Write it before the exception is swallowed. Keep the existing first line. Include no transcript text and no path other than the session id.
4. Decide from the numbers, and write the decision and reasoning in the report. The working rules (not from a source, adjust if the numbers argue otherwise): if the p95 for Stop on a long transcript is over 500 ms, set "async": true on Stop with a "timeout" of 60; and evaluate a debounce that skips the Stop-time export when the session's marker is younger than 120 seconds, since SessionEnd and the 30-minute backfill catch the tail. For SessionEnd keep the hook synchronous and set "timeout": 30, because async survival at SessionEnd is undocumented.
5. Apply the chosen settings with targeted edits. Parse the file afterward and confirm only the Stop and SessionEnd entries differ from the backup.
6. Write scripts/test-session-hook.ps1. Cases: empty stdin, garbage stdin, a missing transcript path, a valid transcript, and a transcript that makes the exporter throw. Each asserts exit code 0 and, where an export was attempted, an export_result line with the right status or a non-empty error. Add it to run-all-tests.ps1's discovery (it picks up test-*.ps1 automatically).
7. Write the check the owner must run for SessionEnd, because a headless run kills async hooks and gives a false result: start a throwaway session in a scratch folder, send one prompt, run /clear, then print the last three lines of jarvis-session-activity.jsonl. An export_result line for that session must appear. Add the check to the report, marked pending. Add a second pending check for the owner: after one normal working day, compute the p50 and p95 of ms from the real activity file.
8. Patch 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration.md (the timeout and async rows) and 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Scripts.md (the hook card and its new output) with dated Corrections entries, and append one line to the Log of 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Layer.md.
</steps>

A syntax-only check, or a check command that failed to start, does not count; if all that is missing is the project's declared dependencies, install them with its own package manager and lockfile, never via sudo or the system package manager, unless told not to. </report>

# P4 - Design Postmortem
Run by Opus 5.5 inside the directory: `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis`
Suggested effort: high. Opus is used here because the task is judgment: finding where a locked design breaks.
Follow this prompting guide to lay it out correctly: `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/[REDACTED]` (written for Sonnet 5.5; the general advice on scope, verification and stopping applies to Opus too, and the Opus 5.5 page lists any difference).
Status of this draft: written before P0 to P3 ran. Refresh the "inputs from earlier phases" list below with what P0 to P3 reported before pasting.
## Prompt - P4 Design Postmortem
````
<role>
You are a skeptical reviewer stress-testing a design before any of it is built. You are paid to find where it fails, not to approve it. You never fix anything in this task; you decide keep, change or cut and you cite evidence for each decision.
</role>

<context>
The owner is building a closed loop for their own Claude Code sessions: capture every session, count what happened, read a sample each week by hand, make one change to how they work, check later whether it helped. The design is in 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Loop Design.md and its layers are L0 Capture (an event hook), L1 Digest (a weekly generated note), L2 Local reviewer (a small local model that flags), L3 Weekly review (the person), L4 Monthly review (the person), L5 the /insights skill. The goal is better output, not scores. The design says it is locked pending this postmortem.

Owner decisions of 2026-10-22: the person reads and codes first and the local model only flags afterward; raw events stay on each machine; the Acer keeps transcripts 60 days; the Acer tree is fresh and tracks Jarvis, The Plan, Home, UMN Courses and any other directory in which Claude Code is used, each with its own project folder; skills and hooks live in the Jarvis vault's .claude folder only; the Dell comes later and covers repo conversations only (Jarvis, internship-research-loop, second-brain-claudekit, portfolio, CausalOps, tradingview, re-exported from live transcripts); Codex, Cursor and other platforms come after Claude Code works end to end on both laptops.

Inputs from earlier phases (refresh before running): the reports of P0 (retention and snapshot), P1 (exporter correctness), P2 (project routing and re-export) and P3 (hook timing). Read their lines in 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Layer.md and the notes they patched.

Seed findings from a context pass on 2026-10-22. They are leads. Check each against the documents and the machine and keep only those that hold; add your own.
1. Per-Prompt Record says the events folder is already ignored by git and Syncthing. .gitignore and .stignore list specific log file names only, so a new events-*.jsonl would be tracked.
2. Hooks matching one event run in parallel and PostToolUse fires concurrently for parallel tool calls, so many processes would append to one JSONL file at once.
3. prompt_id is documented as absent until the first user input; subagent events carry agent_id. The design keys on prompt_id alone.
4. transcript_path may lag the live turn. Anything folded from a transcript at Stop time can be one turn stale.
5. Headless runs (claude -p, including the weekly review and any scheduled Claude run) kill async hooks at teardown. Those sessions would be missing from events.
6. history.jsonl counts typed prompts including slash commands. UserPromptSubmit and UserPromptExpansion are separate events, so a 100 percent coverage line may never be reachable.
7. The weekly note's home in Weekly Review Protocol conflicts with the Note Type Registry (60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/Weekly/ from the AI Weekly Review Template, eight fixed headings) and with Review System's sentence "nothing runs on its own".
8. Nothing schedules the digest or tells the owner a review is due, and nothing proves the whole loop works end to end.
9. agent_lines_kept_7d needs a git-blame replay against commits; the output check could be five hand-opened files.
10. The digest syncs between laptops, so it should carry ids and links, not prompt text. Raw events hold redacted prompt text from every tracked project, including The Plan, and stay local.
11. Every new global hook adds a process per tool call on the owner's machine; the design's cost estimate has no measurement behind it until P3.
12. The design covers Claude Code on the Acer only, but the owner's repos also use Codex and Cursor, and the Dell archive holds a lot of unreviewed material. The design names no path from here to there.

Documents to read, not restate: 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Machine Audit 2026-10-22.md, Conversation Layer Postmortem.md, Conversation Loop Sources.md, Per-Prompt Record.md, Weekly Review Protocol.md, Monthly Review Protocol.md, Local Reviewer.md, Failure Taxonomy.md, and the Build Plan's Corrections section. For hook payloads use the live reference at https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md.
</context>

<goal>
A postmortem note with one finding per layer (L0 to L5) and three cross-cutting findings (schedule and nudge, test and acceptance, platform and machine scope). Each finding has how it could fail, the evidence with a file, count or quoted sentence you opened in this session, and a decision: keep, change or cut. The Design and the Build Plan carry dated Corrections for every change or cut. Anything that needs the owner is a checkbox in Open Questions.
</goal>

<constraints>
- Write only: 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Conversation Loop Design Postmortem.md (type: review, review_kind: audit, status: sprout, following the Audit Report Standard and the audit-report row of the Note Type Registry), dated Corrections in Conversation Loop Design.md and Conversation Loop Build Plan.md, and the next and Open Questions lines of Conversation Layer.md. Leave every other note as it is.
- Build and change nothing: no script, hook, setting or 30_Order Standard, Template or Workflow.
- Every claim cites something you opened this session. List sources you did not open as "not reviewed" in the note. Do not write the postmortem from memory of the design.
- Never open .credentials.json, .env files, history.jsonl, or print transcript text.
- Corrections keep the original text; add a dated "Corrections" layer rather than editing the body.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, update updated:.
- Do not run git commit or git push.
- When the work the user asked for is done and checked, stop and report. Don't add features, tests, files, docs or refactors that weren't asked for. If you think one would help, mention it at the end instead of doing it. Don't start extra rounds of review or launch reviewer subagents.
- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step.
</constraints>

<progress_updates>
Before your first tool call, say in one sentence what you are about to do. After each numbered step, write one line saying how many findings you have. End with a short recap.
</progress_updates>

<steps>
1. Read the context documents and the earlier phases' reports. Re-check the hook payload fields the design depends on against the live hooks reference.
2. Read the Audit Report Standard and its template; use that shape.
3. For each seed finding, test it (open the file, count, or quote the sentence) and write down whether it holds.
4. Write one finding per layer and the three cross-cutting findings. For each, name the failure in one sentence, show the evidence, and decide keep, change or cut. A finding with no way to fail is not a finding; say what would make you change your mind.
5. Write the Corrections in the Design and the Build Plan, including any reorder of later phases, and update the hub's next and Open Questions.
6. List, in the report, what P6, P7 and P8 (event hook, digest, schedules) must change because of your findings. Do not edit those prompts.
</steps>

<verification>
Before you finish, re-open the postmortem and check that every finding has a cited source, a decision, and a way it could be wrong; that every Correction names the finding that caused it; and that no file outside the named ones changed (run git status and compare). Report the result.
</verification>

<report>
Reply with: a table of the twelve or more findings and their decisions; which seed findings held and which did not; the notes you patched with the heading changed in each; what P6 to P8 must change; and the owner decisions you need. Under 400 words.
</report>

# P5 - Review Notes and First Hand Review
Run by Sonnet 5.5 inside the directory: `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis`
Suggested effort: medium. This phase has a human step in the middle; the prompt stops there.
Follow this prompting guide to lay it out correctly: `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/[REDACTED]`
Status of this draft: written before P0 to P4 ran. Run after P2 (so the notes are clean and in the right folders). It can run in parallel with P6 and P7 because the first four weekly reviews are done by hand. Refresh the facts below from the P2 and P4 reports.
## Prompt - P5 Review Notes and First Hand Review
````
<role>
You are preparing and recording the owner's first weekly review of their own Claude Code sessions. The owner does the judging. You prepare the material, keep out of the way during their blind pass, and record what they decide.
</role>

<context>
The weekly review is the part of the loop that makes the owner's sessions better. It works like this, in the owner's own protocol (20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Weekly Review Protocol.md): read about 25 of the week's prompts yourself, write one or two sentences about the first thing that went wrong in each before any model speaks, code each with the Failure Taxonomy, make exactly one change to how you work with Claude Code, and log the change with the check that will say whether it helped. The reason for writing first is evidence: in a controlled study (Buçinca et al., N=199) people who decided before seeing an AI suggestion over-relied on wrong suggestions less (0.64 down to 0.48) than people shown the suggestion with an explanation.

Verified on 2026-10-22: the Note Type Registry sends AI weekly reviews to 60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/Weekly/ using 30_Order/Templates/AI Toolkit/AI Weekly Review Template.md and the Review Standard (30_Order/Standards/Enrich/Review Standard.md). The Standard fixes eight headings: Period Covered, Sources Reviewed, What Ran This Period, Sync & Capture Health, Findings, Decided Fixes, Open Questions, Next Period's Watch List. The protocol's bespoke nine-heading note, saved in 60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Conversations/, does not match; the registry wins. The only existing weekly review is "AI Tools Weekly Review" for 2026-W34 and its file name contains an em dash, which the vault's writing rules ban, so new names use a hyphen. 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Intervention Ledger.md and Failure Taxonomy.md exist (the ledger has no rows; the taxonomy is version 0). The session notes have a "## You" section per typed prompt (redacted); they do not contain slash-command turns or tool-error flags.

To verify first: that after P2 the Acer notes are in their project folders with review_state: raw, and that a "## You" section is enough to code a prompt (open three notes' You sections and decide).
</context>

<goal>
One real weekly review note for the week that just ended, built from the AI Weekly Review Template with no invented headings, containing the sample, the owner's own first-failure notes and codes, the one change, and a row in the Intervention Ledger. The protocol's mapping onto the eight headings is written down. Minutes spent per step are recorded, because they decide the weekly time budget.
</goal>

<constraints>
- Scope: Claude Code sessions on the Acer, all project folders under 60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/Acer/Windows/Claude/ (Jarvis, The Plan, Home, UMN Courses and any other).
- Blind first. Do not write codes, first-failure notes or any judgment about a sampled prompt before the owner has written theirs, and do not describe a prompt as good or bad anywhere in the sample sheet. The point is that the owner's judgment is independent.
- The one change in step 8 is the owner's choice. Apply it only when they tell you to, then log it. Do not add a CLAUDE.md line, skill or hook on your own.
- Write only: the weekly note, the Intervention Ledger row, dated Corrections in Weekly Review Protocol.md, and the Log line in Conversation Layer.md. Use the template as it is.
- Never open .credentials.json, .env files, or history.jsonl.
- Notes: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks, update updated:. Do not run git commit or git push.
- When the work the user asked for is done and checked, stop and report. Don't add features, tests, files, docs or refactors that weren't asked for. If you think one would help, mention it at the end instead of doing it.
- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step. The owner's blind pass in step 5 is the planned stop.
</constraints>

<progress_updates>
Before your first tool call, say in one sentence what you are about to do. After each numbered step, write one line with the result. End with a short recap.
</progress_updates>

<steps>
1. Read the template, the Review Standard, the W34 review and the protocol. In the protocol's "The weekly note" section add a dated Corrections entry mapping each protocol element to the eight headings: the sample sheet and sources under Sources Reviewed; skills, subagents and MCP tools used under What Ran This Period; the capture coverage under Sync & Capture Health; codes, first-failure notes, reconciled flags, bloat and the output check under Findings; the one change, with a link to its ledger row, under Decided Fixes; time spent and unresolved questions under Open Questions; the next review date under Next Period's Watch List. If something has no home, stop and ask.
2. Pick the week: the ISO week that ended most recently (for a run on 2026-10-22 or 23 that is 2026-W42, Monday 19 October's week being W43 and still open; confirm the dates before using them).
3. Create the weekly note from the template with status: seed in 60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Scheduled/Weekly/ named "AI Tools Weekly Review - <year>-W<number>". Fill Period Covered. In Sources Reviewed list the session notes you opened.
4. Build the sample sheet inside Sources Reviewed. Pick about 10 prompts at random across all project folders for that week, with the random seed written in the table caption, and up to 15 flagged prompts chosen by rules you can compute from the notes. Allowed rules, each written in the note beside its results: the next prompt starts with a correction word such as no, don't, wrong, "not what"; the turn is in the top tenth by tokens in its note; the final reply says done or passes and no Bash or PowerShell call follows the last Edit or Write; the turn has the most tool calls in its session. Give each row a link to the session note, the turn number, and the reason it was picked.
5. Stop and hand over. Tell the owner exactly what to do in plain words: read each sampled prompt and what happened, write one or two sentences about the first thing that went wrong, assign codes from the Failure Taxonomy, 15 to 20 minutes, in the Findings section. Wait for them.
6. When they return, copy their notes into Findings as written, count the codes, and run taxonomy upkeep with them: any prompt that fit no code, any definition that shifted. Fill What Ran This Period and the Sync & Capture Health numbers by hand from the notes and 60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/00 - Capture Health.md.
7. Output check: ask the owner to open five things Claude wrote more than a week ago and say how much they changed each. Record their answers under Findings.
8. Ten-minute setup review: ask "what did you correct twice this week?". Help them choose the home for the fix: a hook for a check on one specific tool action, one CLAUDE.md line for a fact that applies every time and cannot be read from the code, a skill for a routine, a subagent for work that floods the window or needs fresh eyes, otherwise a habit. Add one row to the Intervention Ledger with the metric, the baseline from this week's coded rate per 100 prompts, and a review date two weeks out. If nothing recurred, write that and change nothing.
9. Ask the owner for the minutes each step took and record them under Open Questions.
</steps>

<verification>
Before you finish, re-open the weekly note and check: it uses exactly the template's eight headings; every table row links to a real note; the ledger row has a change, a metric, a baseline and a review date; and nothing in the note was judged before the owner's words. Report any check that fails.
</verification>

<report>
Reply with: the mapping table; the sample rules and counts (random and flagged) with the seed; after the owner's pass, the code counts, the change made, the ledger row, and minutes per step; and anything in the notes that made coding hard (a missing field, a dropped turn), since that feeds the event hook and digest phases. Under 350 words.
</report>
````
