# P6 - Event Capture Hook
Run by Sonnet 5.5 inside the directory: `D:\_Anant\20_Progress\Documents\Jarvis`
Suggested effort: high. It registers a global hook that runs on every tool call, so the design must be right.
Follow this prompting guide to lay it out correctly: `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/[REDACTED]`
Status of this draft: designed from the Design note and the hooks reference, before P0 to P4 ran. Do not run it until P3 (timing) and P4 (postmortem) have reported. Refresh the "inputs from earlier phases" part and any step P4 changed.
## Prompt - P6 Event Capture Hook
````
<role>
You are building the sensor of the owner's session-review loop: a small, fast, model-free Node script that records facts about each prompt and tool call in Claude Code. It must never slow the owner down, never print to the session, and never lose a failure silently.
</role>

<context>
The weekly review reads facts, not scraped transcripts. The record shape is defined in 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Per-Prompt Record.md; the design is Conversation Loop Design.md layer L0; the failure codes it must support are in Failure Taxonomy.md. Read those three first. P4's postmortem and its Corrections override this prompt where they differ.

Inputs from earlier phases (refresh): P3's measured cost of a PowerShell hook and its async decision; P4's decisions on the record shape; P2's capture-projects.json routing file and its rules.

Verified in the Claude Code hooks reference on 2026-10-22 (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md; re-check):
- 33 events. Every hook receives session_id, prompt_id (absent until the first user input), transcript_path, cwd, permission_mode, effort, hook_event_name.
- UserPromptSubmit adds prompt. PostToolUse adds tool_name, tool_input, tool_use_id, tool_response, duration_ms. PostToolUseFailure adds error, is_interrupt, duration_ms. PermissionDenied adds reason. SubagentStop adds agent_id, agent_type, agent_transcript_path, last_assistant_message. PreCompact and PostCompact add trigger. InstructionsLoaded adds file_path, memory_type, load_reason. Stop adds last_assistant_message. StopFailure adds error.
- Matching hooks run in parallel and PostToolUse fires concurrently for parallel tool calls. Async hooks cannot change Claude's behavior, and their timeout is not enforced. In headless runs (claude -p) async hooks still running at teardown are killed.
- Stdout of a UserPromptSubmit hook is added to the model's context, so the script must print nothing to stdout.
- Node v24.19.0 is installed. .gitignore and .stignore list specific log file names only; events-*.jsonl is not covered yet.
Owner decisions: raw events stay on each machine (the Acer first). Every directory the owner uses with Claude Code is tracked, with a project name from the same routing file as the exporter. Prompt text is redacted and never pasted documents.
</context>

<goal>
One deterministic hook script and its tests, registered globally for the events that the Per-Prompt Record needs, appending redacted one-line JSON records to a per-machine file that git and Syncthing ignore. A forced failure leaves a failure row. The measured overhead is within P3's budget. The owner can see counts per event for a session and compare them with the prompt count, without reading any text.
</goal>

<constraints>
- The script prints nothing to stdout and always exits 0. It makes no model call and no network call. JARVIS_EVENTS_OFF=1 disables it.
- It never stores a tool_response body (byte length only), a full shell command (first token and an ok flag only), or any prompt over 2,000 characters or 40 lines (store length, the first 200 characters and pasted: true; these two numbers are working rules, not sourced). Redact stored text with the exporter's eight rules, written once in a Node module with test vectors.
- One appendFileSync of one line under 4 KB per event. A failure to write goes to %LOCALAPPDATA%\claude-capture\events-fallback.log.
- Build in 30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/ (the script and a lib folder) with node --test tests beside the existing scripts. Edit only the "hooks" block of C:\Users\anant\.claude\settings.json, with targeted edits after copying it to the scratchpad, and leave the existing Stop and SessionEnd entries alone.
- Add ignore lines for events-*.jsonl and the fallback log to .gitignore and to .stignore (with the (?d) prefix like its neighbors). Prove them with git check-ignore -v. Note that .stignore does not sync, so the Dell needs the same lines by hand later.
- Never open .credentials.json, .env files, or history.jsonl text (counting its lines for a coverage test is allowed).
- Do not run git commit or git push. Notes you patch: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks.
- When the work the user asked for is done and checked, stop and report. Don't add features, tests, files, docs or refactors that weren't asked for. If you think one would help, mention it at the end instead of doing it.
- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step.
</constraints>

<progress_updates>
Before your first tool call, say in one sentence what you are about to do. After each numbered step, write one line with the result. End with a short recap.
</progress_updates>

<steps>
1. Key capture. Register a temporary logger that records only field names and value types per event for about an hour of normal use. Compare with the documented fields, answer whether a slash command fires UserPromptSubmit, and remove the logger. Pin the observed field lists as fixtures in a schema test so a release that renames a field fails a test.
2. Write the script and lib: parse stdin, resolve the project through the routing file's rules (reimplement the rules in Node and share one fixture file of cases with the PowerShell routing test so the two cannot drift), classify the event, redact, append. Common row: version, timestamp, machine, session_id, prompt_id, agent_id when present, event, cwd, project. Per-event fields follow the Per-Prompt Record. On Stop also write a turn_end row with the first 300 redacted characters of last_assistant_message, claims_done (a regex list in an editable JSON config) and verified_after_last_edit (any Bash or PowerShell row after the last Edit or Write row of the same prompt_id whose command head is in an editable list of test, lint and build commands).
3. Failure rows. A parse error or unknown payload writes a failure row with the stage and a truncated message to the same file and a line to stderr. Silence is never an outcome.
4. Tests (node --test, synthetic fixtures): one expected row per event type; empty stdout for every event; garbage and empty stdin give a failure row and exit 0; redaction vectors; routing fixtures; and 50 concurrent processes leave 50 intact lines.
5. Measure: 100 sequential runs, p50 and p95 wall time including Node startup. Compare with P3's budget. If over, investigate before registering.
6. Register after the tests pass: one handler, node "<absolute path>\hooks\jarvis-events.js", async true, timeout 10, for UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure, PermissionDenied, SubagentStop, PreCompact, PostCompact, InstructionsLoaded, StopFailure, and a second Stop entry. Parse the file afterward and confirm only the hooks block changed.
7. Write the smoke test for the owner: open a fresh session in a tracked directory, send one prompt that uses two tools, then run a one-liner that prints row counts per event for that session and the history.jsonl line count for that session id. Mark it pending.
8. Patch Capture Registration, Per-Prompt Record (the ignored-folder claim and the final field list) and the hook table in 30_Order/System/claude-workflow/README.md, each with a dated entry, and add the Log line to Conversation Layer.md.
</steps>

## Prompt - P7 Weekly Digest
````
<role>
You are writing the script that prepares the owner's weekly review: it turns a week of recorded events into a note of counts, flags and a review sample. It judges nothing and uses no model.
</role>

<context>
Layer L1 of Conversation Loop Design.md. The weekly review in Weekly Review Protocol.md opens with a digest: counts per rule flag, the prompts that started with the largest context, the largest tool results, files read twice, which skills, subagents and MCP tools were used and which never were, which instruction files loaded, and one coverage line comparing the prompts typed (history.jsonl line counts) with the prompts that have event rows. Flags and codes are defined in Failure Taxonomy.md: S6 execution error, S7 false self-report, E1 retry loop, E2 context bloat, E3 instruction not loaded, E4 permission friction, E5 capture gap. P6's event rows and Per-Prompt Record.md define the input. The digest syncs between the owner's two laptops.

Verified 2026-10-22: Generated Output Standard (30_Order/Standards/Generated Output Standard.md) wants type: generated, generator, gen_kind (report fits), no status, optional review_state; the generated zone path is undecided, so placement checks are waived. The AI weekly review note it feeds is made in P5 and P8 from the AI Weekly Review Template. transcript_path may lag the live turn, so context sizes for today's prompts can be one turn stale.

Inputs from earlier phases (refresh): the event field list pinned by P6; the sample rules P5 used by hand; P4's decision on agent_lines_kept_7d (deferred unless kept).
</context>

<goal>
A tested script that, given an ISO week, writes the week's digest to 60_Claude/30_Reviews/AI/Conversations/Digests/ with the sections above and the review sample (about 10 random prompts with a printed seed plus up to 15 flagged), as ids and links only. Same input and seed give byte-identical output. A week with no events produces a plain "no events" digest, and a coverage gap is the first line.
</goal>

<constraints>
- No prompt text, no assistant text and no pasted content in the digest: ids, counts, paths relative to the vault, and links to session notes only. Reason: it syncs.
- history.jsonl is read only to count prompts per session and day. Never copy a line.
- Tolerate missing fields and list the Claude Code versions seen. Deterministic, no network, no model call, finish a week in under 30 seconds (a working ceiling).
- One writer per generated note: overwrite the week's digest in place. Thresholds for flags live in an editable JSON config, each with a comment saying it is a working rule.
- Build in 30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/ with tests beside it. Do not edit Standards, Templates or Workflows. Never open .credentials.json or .env files. Do not run git commit or git push.
- Notes you patch: no em dashes, no blank line after a heading, path-qualified wikilinks.
- When the work the user asked for is done and checked, stop and report. Don't add features, tests, files, docs or refactors that weren't asked for. If you think one would help, mention it at the end instead of doing it.
- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step.
</constraints>

<progress_updates>
Before your first tool call, say in one sentence what you are about to do. After each numbered step, write one line with the result. End with a short recap.
</progress_updates>

<steps>
1. Define each flag as code with thresholds in the config. E1: the same tool with a near-identical target three or more times in a row. E2: the largest tool results, files read twice, context size per prompt. E3: InstructionsLoaded rows against an editable list of files expected per project. E4: permission denials. E5: no event rows for prompts counted in history.jsonl. S6: a tool error with no change of approach. S7: claims_done with verified_after_last_edit false.
2. Write the script. Choose the language (Node suits the event file) and say why in one line.
3. Write the sections in this order: coverage line; counts per flag with prompt ids; five largest starting contexts; ten largest tool results; files read twice; tool errors per tool with interrupts counted apart; skills, subagents and MCP tools used and never used (from the installed lists under .claude); instruction files loaded; compactions and permission denials; versions seen; the review sample.
4. Tests: a synthetic events file with known flags and a golden digest; idempotence; missing fields; an empty week; a week with a gap.
5. Validate on real events: give the owner ten sampled prompts with the digest's counts beside them so they can check by hand. Do not mark them equal yourself.
6. Time the run on a week of events.
7. Patch Weekly Review Protocol steps 1 and 2 with a dated entry pointing at the digest path, Capture Scripts with a card, and add the Log line to Conversation Layer.md.
</steps>

<role>
You are making the owner's weekly review prepare itself on a schedule and appear in front of them when it is due, while keeping every scheduled process written down in one timetable.
</role>

<context>
A loop that depends on remembering to run a script dies in week three. The owner wants scheduled runs, and wants every scheduled process tracked with its timing. The review itself stays human: the schedule only prepares the digest and a review skeleton and tells the owner.

Read first: 20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration.md, section "Schedule" (the timetable of all Acer tasks, written 2026-10-22 and maintained from now on), and the existing task pattern: scripts/register-weekly-review-task.ps1, weekly-review-silent.vbs and run-weekly-review.ps1, plus register-capture-backfill-windows-task.ps1 (dry run unless -Apply, refuses other hosts, logs under %LOCALAPPDATA%\claude-capture). The toast code to reuse is in 30_Order/System/sync-workflow/scripts/check-syncthing-status.ps1.

Verified 2026-10-22: the Acer already runs ConversationCapture-Backfill-Windows and Jarvis-GitAutoSync every 30 minutes at :03 and :33, Jarvis-Syncthing-Health every 5 minutes, Jarvis-Codex-Daemon-Guard every 15 minutes (:12, :27, :42, :57), ThePlan-GitSync every 15 minutes (:08, :23, :38, :53), and Jarvis-WeeklyReview on Sundays at 06:00 (it ran on 2026-10-18 at 13:15, late, because the laptop was off at 06:00; the task starts when available). That review covers concept mastery, not conversations. Review System says "nothing runs on its own"; a script that prepares input for a review is not a review, and P4 records the wording conflict.

Inputs from earlier phases (refresh): P7's digest command and output path; P5's weekly note naming; P4's decisions on scheduling; P3's hook settings.

The Schedule section lists every scheduled process with machine, time, purpose, owner script, last verified result and slot rationale, and the new tasks fit into free minutes.
</goal>

<constraints>
- Acer only; the register script refuses other hosts and is a dry run unless -Apply. The scheduled task makes no model call and does not touch Jarvis-WeeklyReview, Jarvis-GitAutoSync or the backfill task.
- New tasks use free minutes (not :03, :33, :08, :23, :38, :53, :12, :27, :42, :57, nor the Syncthing grid). Default: the digest runs Sundays at 21:00 for the current week; the time is a parameter.
- The runner takes a lock, writes logs/conversation-digest.log (add ignore lines for it in .gitignore and .stignore and prove them with git check-ignore -v), and exits non-zero if the digest fails. Create the skeleton only if no note exists; never overwrite an edited note.
- The /startday nudge edits .claude/skills/starting-day/SKILL.md (confirm the path and the Unison sync direction first: read register-jarvis-sync-task.ps1 and sync-jarvis-silent.vbs; if Unison could overwrite the skill, stop and ask).

- Keep working until everything the user asked for is done, and only stop to ask when you can't go on without the user or before a risky step.

</progress_updates>

<steps>

2. Write scripts/run-conversation-digest.ps1: compute the ISO week (parameter -Week to override), call the P7 digest, then create the weekly skeleton from the AI Weekly Review Template with the template placeholders rendered by the script, status seed, Period Covered filled, and a link to the digest. Toast on success and on failure.

### What "complete" means for the Claude Code loop on the Acer
These are the owner's finish conditions of 2026-10-22, each tied to the phase that delivers it.
1. Exported sessions have landed: every session with a live transcript has exactly one note in its project folder. The coverage script says so. (P2)
2. The conversation notes are clean and ready to review: they conform to the Standard, token totals are right, commands are readable. (P1, P2, P10)
3. Logs exist and are checked: the hook's export results, the event rows, Capture Health, the watchdog state, the digest log, the Intervention Ledger. (P3, P6, P8, P9)
4. Scheduled runs work and are written down in one timetable: the digest on Sundays, the watchdog hourly, beside the tasks that already run. (P8, P9, and the Schedule section of [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]])
5. The human review loop has run once for real: a weekly note with the owner's blind pass and one ledger row. (P5)
6. The Obsidian notes are final: Standards, Templates, Workflows, READMEs and maps describe what actually runs. (P10)
7. A test run passed on a real week, including a deliberate break that the watchdog caught. (P11)
8. What is needed for the other platforms and the Dell is written down. (P12)
The loop is running when 1 to 7 are true. It is closed only after the first Intervention Ledger row has been judged on its review date, about two weeks after the first review.
### Phases and prompts
Each prompt is an Agent Prompt note in `Prompts/`: run-by line, guide link, then the whole prompt in one fenced block. The first three are written in full. The next three are detailed and get refined by what the first three return. The last three are drafts, refined as each earlier phase returns.
| Phase | Task | Runs on | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P0 - Retention and Transcript Snapshot\|P0]] | Retention to 60 days and a transcript snapshot | Sonnet 5.5, medium | Setting read back, snapshot counts match, sweep check pending |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P1 - Exporter Correctness\|P1]] | Exporter: prices, frontmatter, encoding, readable commands, one test runner | Sonnet 5.5, medium | All tests green in both shells, two real sessions within 5% |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P2 - Project Routing and Acer Re-export\|P2]] | Project routing file, reroute with approval gate, re-export, coverage script | Sonnet 5.5, high | Coverage script clean, transcript count unchanged |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P3 - Hook Timing and Failure Record\|P3]] | Time the hook, record every export result | Sonnet 5.5, medium | Timing table and `export_result` lines |

Written after the phases above report, because they need their results:
| Phase | Task | Needs first |
|---|---|---|
| P9 | Capture watchdog: its own task, an expected-state file, a kill drill | P3, P6, P8 |
| P10 | Finalize the Obsidian notes: rewrite the conversation Standard, Templates and Workflows from what survived, the AI Conversations README (22 KB, half historical), Conversation Map, Track Conversations, Session Index queries | P4 and everything built |
| P11 | Rehearsal on a real week: coverage, unattended schedule, digest accuracy, blind pass time, kill drill, retention, sync, redaction, overhead, back-out | P0 to P10 and seven days of events |
| P12 | Platform and Dell map, documentation only (next section) | P11 |
After P12: the local reviewer pilot, the `/insights` skill (in the Jarvis `.claude/`), the Dell rollout, then the Codex and Cursor exporters.
### Beyond Claude Code on the Acer, to be known now and built later
The repos the owner works in (internship-research-loop, second-brain-claudekit, portfolio, CausalOps, tradingview, and Jarvis itself) document sessions from more than Claude Code: Codex and Cursor too. Nothing here builds for them yet. P12 writes down, for each platform and each laptop, where its sessions live, the file format, the retention, what the Acer or Dell already holds, and what the exporter and the clean-up of existing material would need. Known on 2026-10-21 and 22: Codex has 44 session files on the Acer in `~/.codex/sessions` (2026-10-13 to 2026-10-21) and no exporter; Cursor's sweep runs only on the Dell and its notes stop at 2026-10-08; Kiro holds configuration, not sessions; Antigravity's conversation store is empty; the Dell archive holds the older and larger body of material and needs a repo-only re-export from live transcripts. The Dell closes its loop after the Acer's, by Jarvis sync plus the build notes, never by hand-copying scripts.

### Numbering: B and P
There was one list, now there is one scheme. The B-numbers (B0 to B10) are the builds in the Sequence table above, written by the research session. The P-numbers are phases, one session prompt each, in `Prompts/`. P replaces B from here on; use P only. Mapping, for reading the older notes:
| Old build | Now |
|---|---|
| B0 retention | P0 |
| B1 exporter fix | P1 (note format) and P2 (project routing and re-export) |
| B2 hook timing | P3 |
| pre-implementation postmortem | P4 |
| B5 review notes | P5 |
| B3 event hook | P6 |
| B4 digest | P7 |
| not in the plan | P8 (schedules and nudge), P11 (rehearsal), P12 (platform and Dell map) |
| B8 dead-man's switch | P9 |
| B10 `30_Order` rewrite | P10 |
| B6 local reviewer pilot, B7 `/insights` skill, B9 Dell rollout | later phases, after P12 |
### What "complete" means for the Claude Code loop on the Acer
These are the owner's finish conditions of 2026-10-22, each tied to the phase that delivers it.
1. Exported sessions have landed: every session with a live transcript has exactly one note in its project folder. The coverage script says so. (P2)
2. The conversation notes are clean and ready to review: they conform to the Standard, token totals are right, commands are readable. (P1, P2, P10)
3. Logs exist and are checked: the hook's export results, the event rows, Capture Health, the watchdog state, the digest log, the Intervention Ledger. (P3, P6, P8, P9)
4. Scheduled runs work and are written down in one timetable: the digest on Sundays, the watchdog hourly, beside the tasks that already run. (P8, P9, and the Schedule section of [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Capture Registration|Capture Registration]])
5. The human review loop has run once for real: a weekly note with the owner's blind pass and one ledger row. (P5)
6. The Obsidian notes are final: Standards, Templates, Workflows, READMEs and maps describe what actually runs. (P10)
7. A test run passed on a real week, including a deliberate break that the watchdog caught. (P11)
8. What is needed for the other platforms and the Dell is written down. (P12)
The loop is running when 1 to 7 are true. It is closed only after the first Intervention Ledger row has been judged on its review date, about two weeks after the first review.
### Phases and prompts
Each prompt is an Agent Prompt note in `Prompts/`: run-by line, guide link, then the whole prompt in one fenced block. The first three are written in full. The next three are detailed and get refined by what the first three return. The last three are drafts, refined as each earlier phase returns.
| Phase | Task | Runs on | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P0 - Retention and Transcript Snapshot\|P0]] | Retention to 60 days and a transcript snapshot | Sonnet 5.5, medium | Setting read back, snapshot counts match, sweep check pending |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P1 - Exporter Correctness\|P1]] | Exporter: prices, frontmatter, encoding, readable commands, one test runner | Sonnet 5.5, medium | All tests green in both shells, two real sessions within 5% |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P2 - Project Routing and Acer Re-export\|P2]] | Project routing file, reroute with approval gate, re-export, coverage script | Sonnet 5.5, high | Coverage script clean, transcript count unchanged |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P3 - Hook Timing and Failure Record\|P3]] | Time the hook, record every export result | Sonnet 5.5, medium | Timing table and `export_result` lines |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P4 - Design Postmortem\|P4]] | Stress test of the loop design | Opus 5.5, high | A finding per layer, each keep, change or cut |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P5 - Review Notes and First Hand Review\|P5]] | Weekly note shape and the first review by hand | Sonnet 5.5, medium, with the owner | Weekly note and one ledger row |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P6 - Event Capture Hook\|P6]] | Event hook | Sonnet 5.5, high | Pinned schema, concurrency and overhead tests |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P7 - Weekly Digest\|P7]] | Weekly digest | Sonnet 5.5, medium | Golden test, owner's ten-prompt hand count |
| [[20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Conversations/Prompts/P8 - Schedules and Review Nudge\|P8]] | Scheduled digest, review skeleton, `/startday` nudge, timetable | Sonnet 5.5, medium | Task result 0, skeleton created once |
Written after the phases above report, because they need their results:
| Phase | Task | Needs first |
|---|---|---|
| P9 | Capture watchdog: its own task, an expected-state file, a kill drill | P3, P6, P8 |
| P10 | Finalize the Obsidian notes: rewrite the conversation Standard, Templates and Workflows from what survived, the AI Conversations README (22 KB, half historical), Conversation Map, Track Conversations, Session Index queries | P4 and everything built |
| P11 | Rehearsal on a real week: coverage, unattended schedule, digest accuracy, blind pass time, kill drill, retention, sync, redaction, overhead, back-out | P0 to P10 and seven days of events |
| P12 | Platform and Dell map, documentation only (next section) | P11 |
After P12: the local reviewer pilot, the `/insights` skill (in the Jarvis `.claude/`), the Dell rollout, then the Codex and Cursor exporters.
### Beyond Claude Code on the Acer, to be known now and built later
The repos the owner works in (internship-research-loop, second-brain-claudekit, portfolio, CausalOps, tradingview, and Jarvis itself) document sessions from more than Claude Code: Codex and Cursor too. Nothing here builds for them yet. P12 writes down, for each platform and each laptop, where its sessions live, the file format, the retention, what the Acer or Dell already holds, and what the exporter and the clean-up of existing material would need. Known on 2026-10-21 and 22: Codex has 44 session files on the Acer in `~/.codex/sessions` (2026-10-13 to 2026-10-21) and no exporter; Cursor's sweep runs only on the Dell and its notes stop at 2026-10-08; Kiro holds configuration, not sessions; Antigravity's conversation store is empty; the Dell archive holds the older and larger body of material and needs a repo-only re-export from live transcripts. The Dell closes its loop after the Acer's, by Jarvis sync plus the build notes, never by hand-copying scripts.
